CursorPapa Cocktail · 15%
Risk Herrington and Greatrex are ice-cold on the board (0-8 / 0-20 T14), which often blunts course specialists.
pace map and likely leadersconfirmed trip of King Of Chaos's 17 Jul Wolverhampton winMaris Angel fitness after 59-day layoff
Papa Cocktail is the most grounded 6f play: CD 2-10 at Wolverhampton (AW), three wins at the trip overall, and two tight recent Doncaster six-furlong efforts (2nd, 5th) say he is running to his mark. Draw 10 fits a track where high stalls have matched mid over the last six years (~35%), and OR 78 off 9-9 is fair in a race where the favourite looks flattered. King Of Chaos is the main danger — he bolted up here 14 days ago under in-form Cieren Fallon (773171) — but D 0-9 and stall 1 (low-draw bucket ~29%) keep him behind on suitability despite the momentum.

The biggest threat to Papa Cocktail is not ability but the saddle: a 7yo needs a sharp booking, and this yard/rider pair brings no recent heat. If Herrington/Greatrex cannot settle him from a wide draw in a 10-runner handicap, King Of Chaos or draw-neutral HK Fourteen (beaten 0.3L at York, 2-8 at 6f, 2-6 on AW, Carroll/De Sousa) can overtake without needing a career best. American Style at 3.4 is the fade — 12th of 13 last time, 1-14 at six furlongs, and Kevin Ryan's 0-18 T14 do not support a 30% market share.

CursorJoga Bonito · 24%
Risk Still 0-2 on Good and only three runs in a messy 18-runner maiden.
pace map and possible Slattery/JPOB pacemaker intentmarket drift on debutants between now and offwhether Ascot form translates for Ischgl at 7f
Joga Bonito is the most reliable form horse in the field: third at Down Royal over this trip, beaten a neck off 2.0, reads clearly above Supreme Act and Closefield Beau from that day; before that he was second at Fairyhouse with a useful Twomey/Lee combination in respectable form. The 38% model figure is too high for an 18-runner 2yo event, but he still leads on proven 7f ability, progressive figures, and top-tier connections; market favourite Otherworldly looks short on regression after a Class 1 sixth over 6f, low-draw stats here, and no 7f proof.

Ischgl is the main danger: OR 80, draw 16 suits Galway’s high-draw 7f bias, and the Ascot “17/25, beaten 0.3L” line is better than the bare position; Listowel second adds substance. He splits with Joga Bonito on unproven Good-ground form and a colder Murphy/Coen yard, and debuts such as Heart Of You (Harrington/Foley, poor draw 1) and Petrolio (Meade, strong RTF) are live if the race collapses into a breeding lottery.

CursorFinn Ironside · 20%
Risk Draw 1 sits in Newcastle's weakest 7f AW band (26% winners vs 40% high).
pace map / early speedtactical match-ups in a 9-runner fieldwhether Standard-to-Slow AW suits each runner equally
Finn Ironside gets the nod mainly on suitability: 3-14 at today's distance, course-and-distance 1-6, and a fresh Southwell (AW) 7f run beaten only 0.8L — the closest direct analogue on the card. He is 2lb lower on the ratings than Holly Mist with a useful 5lb claim, and recent form (4-3-8-7-4-5) is more dependable than the joint-favourite's patchier 3-4-5-5-3-6 line. Draw 1 is a real negative on a track where high stalls win 40% of the time, but AW proven form outweighs that for me here.

The biggest threat is Holly Mist: top OR 64, Roger Varian/Jack Mitchell (strong six-month ranks), draw 7 in the favoured high third, and she should strip fitter off 27 days. I cannot endorse the model's 59% — that far exceeds handicap reality and your calibration band (≥0.40 claims win ~41%) — because her AW/distance record is still 0-3 with no Newcastle form, echoing the Pandemonium post-mortem risk on unproven AW/slow profiles. Into The Light heads my estimates but nowhere near the model’s 45%. Draw 2 is useful on this track, the Appleby/Buick pairing is live (Buick’s recent Goodwood record supports that), and the figures stack up: close second at Sandown on good over 10f, and at Ascot over 12f he was beaten only 0.3L in a deep C2 — stronger company than most rivals have faced. He profiles as a prominent racer (p1) with an 84% rtf rating, so fitness looks solid. On ability and connections he is the most likely winner, but in a 14-runner 3yo handicap that is still only roughly a one-in-six chance.

The main danger is structural, not price: he carries 88 with Pierian and Echo Of Stars while Mythical Valentine (79), Astracornus (74), and Devil’s Peak (82) are much better treated by the weights, and several have already won at 12f on good/good-to-firm. Mythical Valentine is the clearest alternative — Murphy/Balding in form, a Windsor 12f GF win, and 1-1 on today’s going — while a hot Astracornus and last-time-out winner Devil’s Peak make this a genuinely fragmented race. I would not treat 3.6 as proof; the fair win chance is closer to 17% than the market or model imply.

CursorAngel Of England · 12%
Risk 0-10 at Newcastle and a crowded prominent pace from several rivals.
sectional pace dataBrittain dual-runner stable intentfinal going reading on the day
Angel Of England is the most coherent profile in a messy 14-runner handicap. He maps well to Newcastle’s six-furlong AW bias (high draws ~43% of winners; he is dr10), runs prominently which suits this surface, and arrives sharp off six days: third over this track’s six furlongs beaten only 0.3L, with a prior Ripon second. OR 50 keeps him in the right grade band, and the 8.2 price looks closer to fair ability than the favourite’s.

The main worry is that he is still a course maiden (0-10 here) in a race where Uncle Sam (dr14, CD 1-4) and No News (recent AW second) both have claims, while Long Shot and others can compromise the front-running plan. That keeps true win chance in low double digits rather than solid favourite territory — hence no strong model-style conviction.

CursorGemini Man · 26%
Risk 13f is a furlong further than his June Musselburgh win and he carries joint-top weight.
pace map and early speedwhether Good is riding quick or holdingany unseen stable confidence behind Storm On Jura's price
Gemini Man is the most substantiated winner profile: he has already won at Musselburgh this summer (12f, Good), Kevin Stott is riding well, draw 2 suits a 13f handicap here, and his OR 53 is less worrying when the apparent favourite is a winless nine-time runner on 0-6 Good with three poor runs in a row. Sophiesticate is the main danger on course-and-distance credentials (2-4 at Musselburgh, 2-4 CD), draw 1, and a close third behind Gemini Man over this track in June; Jedhi Knight and the lightly raced Falaise Blanc fill out the place claims. Storm On Jura at 1.1 looks like market noise rather than ability — no career win, no going or course form, and recent figures of 8-6-9 do not justify a 90%+ implied chance.

The biggest risk to Gemini Man is stamina and weight at a trip he has not won over, plus a fresh 9th on AW last week (though only 0.2L back). If the race develops into a crawl and something gets an easy lead, Sophiesticate's local knowledge and low draw could overturn him in a tight finish.

CursorSexy Chick · 19%
Risk Draw 2 is in the weakest third of the July 6f draw profile, and she must beat fresh elite-yard debutants.
Pace map with multiple unraced or lightly raced speed typesStrength of the winner she narrowly missed here over this C&DWhether Louis The Fifth's 5f form transfers cleanly to 6f
**Sexy Chick** is the pick on hard evidence, not market rank. She is the only runner with a live course-and-distance marker: second of three here over this exact 6f Good To Firm Class 4 trip, beaten only 0.3L. That beats debut hype and short prices elsewhere. The 5lb filly allowance helps against colts on 9-7, Robert Havlin knows the July track, and her 7f run reads as stamina-positive rather than a negative. At 7.4 the market (~13.5%) undershoots a fair win chance nearer 19% in a seven-runner field.

The main risk is the draw and the unknowns around her. Stall 2 sits in the low third where only ~31% of July 6f winners come from, while **National Honour** (dr7, high-bias zone) brings Charlie Appleby, an 84% RTF Wootton Bassett sire line, and the strongest trainer profile in the race. **Hayate** adds Roger Varian’s hot yard despite a poor draw, and **Blue Victory** pairs Marco Ghiani’s strong recent record with a helpful high draw. Sexy Chick has shown she belongs at this trip; she still has to prove she is now the best horse in the race, not merely close to it.

Room Fourteen is the most reliable profile on form and suitability: back-to-back wins at Doncaster (10f, GF) and Redcar (10f, G), a close second at Ascot over this trip, a 3-5 distance record and 2-3 on good ground, with Ryan Moore on a horse clearly effective at a mile and a quarter. The Class 2 step-up is real, but the recent figures are strong and consistent rather than a single spike; he looks fairly treated at OR 83 and is not a short-price illusion like the favourite.

The biggest threat is Zgharta: draw 7 sits in the high-draw band that dominates Goodwood 10f handicaps, and Oisin Murphy on an Andrew Balding runner is a top-tier combination. Chester’s close fourth over 10f on good ground confirms trip viability, even if Ascot form was patchy. Silver Lake is too short at 2.6 for me — only three runs, nothing beyond 8f, no course/distance or going bank — and Lady Dora Mae’s Goodwood second is appealing with Billy Loughnane, but stall 2 fights the course draw stats at the weights.

Rexem gets the nod on profile rather than price: he’s the most reliably completing chaser near the top of the weights, with back-to-back Good-ground runs at Kilbeggan (2nd by 0.3L, LR7, then 4th beaten less than a length two weeks prior). The 8.0 quote looks roughly fair, not proof of superiority; I have him ~10% in a wide 22-runner handicap, not a standout ≥25% type.

Charlie Luciano is the main danger: he won convincingly over 26f on Good at Kilbeggan (LR21), which answers stamina questions better than Rexem’s 20f races, and Meade/Stone-Walsh are in respectable recent form. Sheer Beauty gets the nod mainly on course evidence and draw: she is the only runner with a Bath win (1-1 at the track), sits in the high-draw third where ~38% of ~6f winners come from, and shares top official rating (68) despite one start. That is a sharper local fit than Tricky Jenny, who is favorite off three winless runs including a poor AW latest, or Havana Gift, who is progressive but still 0-4 over 5f. Havana Gift is the main danger — draw 7 is the best stall on the bias map, her 47242 line is the most reliable form in the field (close second at Nottingham 5f on good to firm), and she looks the horse most likely to improve past a one-time course winner if ability not context decides it.

The biggest risk to Sheer Beauty is sample size: one run is a thin base for a 26% win claim, she carries the most weight in the race, and the Birkett/Hogan team is far less prolific than Owen or Quinn yards that could propel rivals. If Tricky Jenny rebounds off James Owen’s hot stable form, or Texas Crown/Thai Boxer debut with more ability than their limited market prices imply, Sheer Beauty’s edge narrows quickly.

Gerry's Darling looks the most solid winner profile on exposed evidence. She was a clear second at Redcar over this trip in the same class on similar ground, while Hey Up Ronnie was only third in that field despite shorter SP; she also takes 5lb off the main rivals and Kevin Stott arrives in better recent form than most in the race. Draw four sits in the stronger Musselburgh 7f zone (high/mid bands outperform low), which helps against Ronnie on two and debutante Coul Blue on one.

The main reservation is that her Redcar leap came after two poor runs in hotter Class 2 company at Doncaster, so the true ability level is still uncertain rather than proven. Hey Up Ronnie remains the main danger: his York close fifth in Class 3 hints at underlying class, his sire stats are stronger, and he may improve with a sharper pace setup, but the Redcar form, extra weight, and low draw keep him narrowly behind on today's facts.

CursorNight Moon · 13%
Risk Mullins has Thread Of Gold with W J Lee, so Night Moon may be the stable's secondary string despite the better draw and trip profile.
pace map and Mullins tactical plan with two runnersImmutable fitness off 89 daysexact Curragh 12f winner form from 28 June
Night Moon is the profile horse the market is underweighting. He won over 14f on good ground at Fairyhouse last month, so stamina is proven; he carries a workable OR 85 from draw 14, right in Galway’s high-draw zone (45% of 12f winners). That matters in an 18-runner handicap where several rivals share the key June Curragh 12f form line. W P Mullins is the card’s top trainer, and Night Moon’s recent target was a proper staying test rather than Thread Of Gold’s 9f win, which is less reassuring at a mile and a half here.

The main risk is yard preference: Lee rides the shorter-priced stablemate from a mid draw, which often signals the main contender even when Night Moon looks better suited on paper. Dawn Rising is the main danger — only half a length off in that Curragh 12f good-ground race despite finishing eighth, and today’s high draw should suit him far better. The Real Screamer has the cleanest 12f good form but draw 1 fights the course bias badly; Navy Waters and Fixation are solid each-way profiles without quite the same upside.

Symbol Of Hope is the most grounded winner candidate without trusting the market favourite. She has two Bath wins from twelve course starts, a firm-ground win record (1-3), and was only 0.2L third in this same field over six furlongs here on 06-05 — the strongest course-and-reopp profile in the race. Draw six is neutral-to-fair on a card where high stalls win disproportionately, and Callum Hutchinson’s recent strike rate is respectable; the 44 OR is not a negative when her Bath form repeatedly exceeds that mark. Sisters In The Sky is next: draw eight suits the documented high-draw bias, she has firm form (1-6) and was 0.1L fourth in that reopp, but she has only one course win versus Symbol’s two and finished behind her last time.

The main risk is that this is a compressed handicap, not a clear class gap — Jackie Brown, Spirited Dancer, Some Nightmare and I Was Thinking were all in that June Bath race within a length or so, so Symbol can run her race and still find one or two improvers. CursorAmantha · 24%
Risk Draw 1 sits in Musselburgh’s weakest 7f zone and Liveadream already knows how to finish close here.
pace map with Makin’s two runnersAmantha’s winning draw last timewhether Bluestone Lady’s AW progress transfers to Good turf
Amantha is the most reliable form anchor: she has already won over this exact course, distance and going (7f Good at Musselburgh on 22 June), beating Liveadream in that field, and her OR/weight profile still looks competitive in a modest Class 6. The market has overplayed Bigalo on top rating alone — his last three runs (7th/9th/6th, including a remote York effort) do not justify favouritism — while Aurora Valkyrie’s Ayr third over 7f Good and Bluestone Lady’s high-draw slot are fair alternatives but less proven on today’s specific test.

The biggest threat to Amantha is structural rather than ability-based: low draws win only ~23% of Musselburgh 7f handicaps here, and Brian Ellison/Ben Robinson arrive cold (0-25 / 0-16 in the last fortnight). Liveadream’s narrow defeat on this track is a live danger if she rebounds from the Thirsk flop, and Bluestone Lady’s draw-7 advantage plus recent Newcastle AW improvement make her the likeliest horse to exploit bias if Amantha does not jump cleanly.

CursorHonky Tonk Girl · 26%
Risk Only two runs and the AW flop shows she is not yet a reliable sprint profile.
Kodicall debut abilityNzuri early-pace scenarioElmfield freshness off 35 days
Honky Tonk Girl is the most substantiated winner on the evidence, not the shortest price alone. Her Nottingham third over 6f on good firm (Class 4, beaten 1.5L) is the clearest trip-and-going form in the field and steps her down in class today; draw six also sits in Bath’s strongest high-draw band (38% of winners). Nzuri is more exposed but 0-5 at the distance and a front-running profile are negatives, while Kodicall is a debut with a useful Kodiac/Soldiers Call profile and Taylor Fisher in fair form but no proof at 5.5f on firm from draw one (weaker zone). Elmfield is the main danger: George Scott is the best-connected trainer on the card, her 76% rtf is the highest here, and she was only 0.2L off the winner at Doncaster over six furlongs on good firm in this class — but she has had 35 days off and is 0-2 at today’s distance.

The biggest risk to Honky Tonk Girl is how narrow her profile still is. One good turf sprint and one poor AW run over seven furlongs is a small sample, Mason Paetel is 0-0 in the last 14 days, and Oliver Cole’s yard is cold; at 2.3 the market is pricing her nearer 40% than the ~26% I would assign. If Elmfield is fully fit, or Kodicall runs up to pedigree on…

Where's Freddy is the most reliable win angle on form, not price. He won this exact July course, seven furlongs, Good to Firm, Class 4 handicap thirteen days ago under Marco Ghiani, with strong course-and-distance figures (3-4 CD, 4-6 C) and a jockey riding well lately. That beats theoretical ability: he has already solved this puzzle recently, whereas several market leaders are less proven here.

I disagree with Exposure as a 50% chance. One career start, draw one in a race where high draws win 45% of the time, and no wins at trip or going make that far too bullish despite Gosden/Havlin and respectable 8f form. Cherry Cobbler is the main danger — second over this C/D in June, draw seven suits the bias, and she looks well treated on OR — with Jumby next after finishing second to Freddy here on 18 July, though top weight and a wide run are headwinds. Dion Baker's hot streak came in weaker Class 6 company at Brighton; the class rise caps him.

Only One Scobie is the clearest trip-and-class match: he has already won an 8f Leopardstown handicap off a big price, sits on the best OR (70) with a high draw (16) in a race where high stalls have won 40% of Galway ~8f races, and his latest 3rd over 7f says he is race-fit. Femme Beauty (draw 19, good-ground winner, Ballinrobe winner) is the likeliest beneficiary if the front-runners overdo it; Hassaniya and Aquilla Star are the other live contenders if you want place coverage rather than a single win bet.

This is an open five-runner Class 6 sprint where no runner clears a staking threshold once draw, going, and course form are weighed together. Beerwah is the marginal top pick on merit: draw 5 suits Bath's high-draw lean (38% of winners), he carries competitive weight off OR 62, has a fresh 5f win at Nottingham on good ground, and a useful 3-22 distance record. Candy Warhol runs him close on consistency — 70% RTF, stacked 2nd/4th placings, and two distance wins — but draw 2 is less ideal and recent form is AW-heavy on a firm turf sprint. Nordic Glory is the course-and-distance angle (1-3 CD, won Bath 5f firm in May) but draw 1 sits in the weakest third of the bias table and 39 days off with a Windsor flop caps enthusiasm. Connie's Rose is top-rated but 0-13 at Bath and all recent work at 7f; Payforanother Daay is exposed here but 0% RTF and well held overall.

The biggest risk to Beerwah as the notional winner is suitability and freshness: zero firm-ground wins, last start eighth at Beverley beaten only 1.2L but from a poor position in the race narrative, and the 2.5 price implies ~40% — roughly sixteen points above a calibrated true chance near 24%. Dropping into a 0-75 with a workable mark looks right; the 21-day refresh is a plus. That profile supports a favourite — but not a 57% one in a five-runner handicap.

Society Girl is the main danger: beaten a neck over 12f on good/firm at Epsom last time, with Ed Walker’s yard in strong form — though she is lightly raced, still seeks a win at trip/going, and also sits on the wrong side of the draw. Draw 2 suits the low-draw angle on this card, and J M Sheridan is a solid booking. Counting Coup from draw 1 is an improver but only two starts and a soft-ground 13f win is a big extrapolation; Way Down South looks opposable on 0-4 good and 0-2 course form despite OR 100 and W J Lee.

CursorThornaby Pearl · 27%
Risk Trainer and jockey are 0-9 and 0-0 in the last 14 days, so the market favourite may be running on stale connection form.
pace map and early speedGoldie yard preference between two runnersintra-day going/shift on Good ground
Thornaby Pearl gets the nod mainly on draw and profile, not because 3.6 looks generous. Stall 6 sits in the high-draw band that has supplied roughly half of Musselburgh 5f winners over the last six years, against only about one in five from low stalls; that is a material edge in a six-runner sprint. He shares top weight on OR 64, arrives off a Good-ground win at Pontefract and a narrow second at Hamilton, and his 3-17 distance record says the trip is fine despite zero course form. At a true win chance around 27%, he is roughly fair at 3.6 rather than a standout overlay.

Golden Havana is the main danger. Michael Dods and Connor Beasley are the hottest combination on the card, and three consecutive seconds — including two on Good ground within the last week — mark him as the most reliable improver. Draw three is the problem: it cuts against this track’s strong high-draw bias, and he lacks Musselburgh experience. Wee Mary and Thunderstorm Katie both have solid course-and-distance claims for Jim Goldie but are badly positioned in stalls one and two; South Parade’s CD 1-3 is offset by dreadful recent form; Peregrine Falcon’s stall five helps but she reads as an AW-placed outsider on…

Maxi King is the clearest stayer in the field on exposed form: he has already handled 22f at Ascot in the same June Class 2 staying handicap where Berkshire Sundance weakened, and his Chester second over 18f was a narrow defeat in stronger company. The 9-12 and OR 103 are negatives, but they reflect ability rather than a soft mark, and Toby Moore’s claim is a small help in a race where many rivals are stepping up sharply from 16f or shorter.

The biggest danger is that this remains a wide, hard-to-forecast Cup with no dominant profile—Believitanducan’s three staying wins and strong Newcastle handicap form make him the main alternative, especially off a lower mark, though draw 16 in an 18-runner is a serious Goodwood headwind. Valiancy looks short on trip evidence at 20.5f, and I would not treat his 3.8 quote as proof of superiority over proven stayers.

Foxy Night looks the most reliably pointed at this trip: highest known OR (77), prominent run-style on AW, and a narrow second over 7f Standard at Southwell eight days ago (0.3L, beaten only a short-priced rival). That is stronger recent evidence than Rajiba’s two-run profile, where the best work is over 8f here and the 6f fourth reads less ideal for today’s middle distance. Draw bias at Wolverhampton ~7f is mild (mid slightly best); Foxy Night’s stall 6 sits in that favourable band without the high-draw penalty Star Of Commala faces.

Rajiba is a fair second case on course familiarity and Cieren Fallon’s strong recent record, but the 2.5 quote looks driven by connections and one solid 8f run rather than proven 7f winning form. Gilding The Lily is the main upset threat from the Gosden yard with a speed pedigree and a sensible mid-low draw, while Esplanade’s rail draw helps a Charles Hills debut — but four unrated newcomers and Marianne Mozart’s long layoff keep this a low-confidence contest, hence a modest 20% win claim rather than a strong staking opinion.

That run beats Wicklow Way’s recent Newcastle evidence (ninth over 8f, ninth at Wolverhampton over 10f) on trip-specific form, and Whillans (3-8 in 14 days) is the sharper trainer angle. CursorTalk Of New York · 33%

Talk Of New York is the logical winner on figures and profile: top OR (116), two wins at 8f (2-2), a clear Sandown Group-race success over this trip on good ground, and the Appleby/Buick team in respectable form. He is the most likely winner, but a fair probability is nearer one-third than the model’s 82% or the market’s ~63% — this is a seven-runner Class 1 with several rivals already competitive at the same level.

The main danger is Shayem, not the market rank. He won Newmarket (July) over 8f on good to firm in Class 1 (OR 109, only seven runs), arrives fresher (22 days), and has draw 6 where Goodwood’s 8f bias favours high stalls (~40% of winners). Saber Strike is the other major threat after close defeats in big-field Ascot and York Class 1 races; Catullus adds each-way interest from draw 5 and course form but looks secondary to Talk Of New York in the Appleby yard. I would not stake the favourite at 1.6; Shayem and Saber Strike are the value against an overbet short price.

Siouxperb is the most likely winner on ability alone — a convincing Yarmouth 6f Class 4 win is the clearest form in the field, and Archie Watson/Oisin Orr are solid connections — but I rate him around 27%, well below the 2.5 quote (~40%). Nothing here clears my value bar.

Stand Strong is the most race-fit profile on the data: CD 1-3 and course form of second and sixth at Newcastle AW over 16f, both beaten only 0.3L, plus draw 8 in a zone where high draws have taken 45% of winners over six years at this trip. He carries 9-1 against 9-9 topweights, so the handicap gives him a useful edge over Chambers and Time Turner on OR. Galahad Threepwood from the same yard on draw 1 may soften the pace up front, which suits a stalker with proven Newcastle stamina.

The biggest risk is freshness and current form: 46 days off and a fifth of eight at Catterick on his last start are not ideal prep, and he is not a recent winner unlike Chambers. Chambers (OR 64, recent win, Mitchell in fair form) is the main danger but draw 6 fights the bias and he is 0-2 at trip with no Newcastle record. Is She Now looks short at 4.7 on D 0-11 with a low draw stepping up from 11–13f — I have her nearer 11% true win chance than the market implies. Ned Broy (draw 9) and Time Turner (course winner but draw 3 and untested at 16.5f) fill out the place claims; the rest are single-digit win chances in a wide-open Class 6.
